Also the lock issue persists.\n\nThe real problem seems to be a lock contention issue specific to this filesystem/OS. Let me try a completely different approach: \n1. Kill the process\n\n\n2. Clear all locks and incomplete files\n3. Download model files sequentially using Python's `hf_hub_download` instead of `snapshot_download` to avoid parallel downloads\n\nI could set environment variables like `HF_HUB_DISABLE_PROGRESS_BARS=1` and `HF_HUB_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT=600`, or use the Python API with `max_workers=1` to disable parallel downloads entirely. The locks are being held by threads within the same process, which should work fine normally—unless the filesystem doesn't support proper file locking, which could be the case with NFS or FUSE mounts on AutoDL. I could try disabling file locking entirely or using a non-shared directory instead. Let me check if there's a way to disable the `FileLock` mechanism or adjust its timeout settings in the huggingface_hub library.\n\nActually, the real issue is that the AutoDL data disk is likely a network or FUSE mount that doesn't support `fcntl.flock()` properly on Linux. The simplest fix would be to set the lock directory to a local tmpfs, or better yet, use `TMPDIR=/tmp` to download to the local disk first where file locking works correctly, then move the files to the data disk. If that doesn't work, I could kill the process, clear any remaining locks, and run a sequential download script without parallel
